- **obk-handoff**: Stopping work in a state someone else can pick up: finishing or backing out of the step you are in, making what you have durable, and writing the note a cold reader needs (what this is for, what is done, what is proven, where it sits, and what to do first). Run it when you are asked to: "hand this over", "write it up for whoever picks this up", "pause here and write it up", "we are out of time". - **obk-recall**: Rebuilding where a piece of work actually stands: fixing the scope first, reading back over your own history and whatever the project records around the same code, checking what you find against the live state, and handing back a short brief of where things are and what to do next. Run it when you are asked to: "catch me up", "what was I doing with X", "where did I leave off", "get up to speed on this before we start". Not something to do unbidden: being asked to carry on with a task is a request for the task. ([guwenqing/orca-bot-kit](https://github.com/guwenqing/orca-bot-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/obk-recall))
- **obk-researching**: Finding something out from sources and saying how well you actually know it: going to the source that owns a claim rather than a write-up of it, keeping track of what you searched as well as what you found, marking every claim by how it is supported, surfacing the sources that disagree, and saying plainly what you could not find out.
